# The compare suite: results

Generated by `report.py` from the CSV files beside it. Times are ms per query, one thread on one pinned core: the median over queries of each query's median of its repetitions, then `/` the p90 over the same per-query medians. Under a visible set, faiss filters through an `IDSelectorBitmap` and PDX through a selection vector; `(compacted)` is faiss over a copy of the visible rows alone. Exactness (exact k-NN, threshold) is from `check.py`: no mark means every answer was exact; `~` that some items differ but all lie within the rounding allowance; `WRONG n` counts items beyond it; `?` not checked. Filters: `none` is the whole corpus; `random-p` a random visible set of p% (k-NN and threshold shares are then of the visible set).

**Machine** (`machine.json`, recorded at the last run): AMD Ryzen 9 5900X 12-Core Processor, 12 cores (avx2, fma), 47.0 GiB RAM, 12.0 GiB swap, 54.1 GiB free on the data disk, kernel 6.18.58.1-microsoft-standard-WSL2, virtualisation: wsl2; recorded 2026-11-22 10:24:08 GMT

## Approximate k-NN at fixed recall

Each cell is the median ms per query of the fastest grid point whose mean recall@10 reaches the target (no interpolation between grid points), then in brackets that time over vexact's: above 1, vexact is that many times faster; below 1, the other system is faster. `max r` when the system's grid never reaches the target: r is the best recall it reached. Empty: not run under that filter.

### At a glance: vexact against faiss HNSW and against the fastest other system

Each cell: `HNSW ratio / fastest other ratio (system)`, each the other system's time over vexact's at that recall (above 1: vexact is faster). `-` when the system never reaches the recall; `vexact -` when vexact does not.
